\displaystyle\sum_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\sum_{k}\sum_{l}\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}\Big(\frac{1}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(s)/\widetilde{\rho}_{l}~\mathrm{d}s\Big)+o_{n}(1)

(45)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\sum_{k}\sum_{l}\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}\Big([h(y_{i}^{n})]_{l}/\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\Big)+o_{n}(1)

where
h(\cdot)
is the solution to
\text{Opt}(r)
(
7
)
and

y_{i}^{n}:=\sum_{l}\frac{1}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(s)~\mathrm{d}s=\frac{1}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{W}_{+}^{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s.

By
\bm{W}_{+}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{W}_{+}
and the continuity of
\bm{W}_{+}
in
Proposition
1
, applying the mean value theorem for integrals yields the
existence of
\zeta_{i}\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]
such that

y^{n}_{i}=\frac{1}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{+}(s)\mathrm{d}s=\frac{1}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{W}_{+}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)=\bm{W}_{+}(\zeta_{i})+o_{n}(1).

(46)

We use continuity of
h(\cdot)
to complete the proof of (
6
). For any
r\geq 0
,
although
\text{Opt}(r)
can potentially have multiple optimal solutions, it
suffices to study properties of one specific optimal solution.

Lemma 14

(Properties of the optimal allocation)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
, and
4
hold. Let
\underline{h}(0)=\mathbf{0}
and for any

r>0
, let
\underline{h}(r)
be the solution to the following equations

\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{x_{l}}{\widetilde{\rho}_{l}}\Big)=\widetilde{\mu}_{m}\widetilde{C}_{m}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{x_{m}}{\widetilde{\rho}_{m}}\Big),~\forall~l,m\in[K];\quad\sum\limits_{l=1}^{K}x_{l}=r;\quad x_{l}\geq 0,~\forall~l\in[K].

(47)

Then, i) for any
r>0
, there exists a unique solution, ii)

\underline{h}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{K}
is continuous, iii) for any

r\geq 0
,
\underline{h}(r)
is an optimal solution to
\text{Opt}(r)
(
7
).

See Section
D.3
for the proof.

By Lemma
14
and uniform continuity of
\underline{h}
and
C_{k}

on compact sets (Assumption
4
),

\displaystyle\lim\inf_{n}\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{$\pi_{n}$}}(t;Q^{n})\geq

\displaystyle\liminf_{n}\sum_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\sum_{k}\sum_{l}\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}\Big([h(y_{i}^{n})]_{l}/\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\sum_{k}\sum_{l}\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}\Big(\big[h\big(\bm{W}_{+}(\zeta_{i})\big)\big]_{l}/\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\Big).

Note that the function

\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}([h(\bm{W}_{+}(\cdot))]_{l}/\rho_{l})
is continuous and
thus Riemann integrable. Letting
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
results in (
6
):

\lim\inf_{n}\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{$\pi_{n}$}}(t;Q^{n})\geq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{K}\int_{0}^{t}\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}C_{k}\Big(\frac{\big[h\big(\bm{W}_{+}(s)\big)]_{l}}{\widetilde{\rho}_{l}}\Big)\mathrm{d}s.

To show (
8
), consider feasible p-FCFS policies
\{\pi_{n}^{\prime}\}
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the original processes under
\mathbb{P}^{n}
satisfy
\mathbb{P}^{n}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{$\pi$}_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x]=\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{$\pi$}_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x],~\forall~x\in\mathbb{R},t~\in[0,1]
, according to
the Skorohod representation. By Fatou’s lemma, for any

x\in\mathbb{R},t\in[0,1]
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim\inf_{n}\mathbb{P}^{n}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\pi_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x]=\lim\inf_{n}\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{$\pi$}_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}}[\lim\inf_{n}\mathbb{I}\{\bm{J}^{n}_{\pi_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x\}].

As
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bm{J}^{n}_{\pi_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})\geq\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q)

\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}
-a.s. by (
6
), we have that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}}[\lim\inf_{n}\mathbb{I}\{\bm{J}^{n}_{\pi_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x\}]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}}[\mathbb{I}\{\lim\inf_{n}\bm{J}^{n}_{\pi_{n}^{\prime}}(t;Q^{n})>x\}]\geq\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q)>x].

Combining equations above yields (
8
)
for any feasible p-FCFS policies. We can further extend (
8
) to any feasible policies using
Lemma
11
. This completes our proof.

Proof of Claim
1

Since

n^{-1}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(n\cdot)\rightarrow\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}e
by Proposition
3
,

\displaystyle n^{-1}[\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt_{i+1})-\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt_{i})]=\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})+o_{n}(1).

(48)

Apply the convergence (
48
) to
rewrite
\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{kl,i}^{n}}[\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{kl,i}^{n}}[\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}]=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\big(n^{-1}[\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt_{i+1})-\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt_{i})]\big)^{-1}\int_{nt_{i}}^{nt_{i+1}}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl},

(49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1}\big[[\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})]^{-1}+o_{n}(1)\big]\int_{nt_{i}}^{nt_{i+1}}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl},

where the last line holds since

(x+\Delta x)^{-1}=x^{-1}-\Delta x+o(\Delta x)
.

We approximate
\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}
using a
variant of Little’s Law that we prove in
Section
D.4
.

Proposition 5

(Little’s law)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose
Assumptions
1
,
2
, and
3
hold. Then, for
any
0\leq a<b\leq 1

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=o(1),~\forall~k,l\in[K],

(50a)

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=o(1),~\forall~l\in[K].

(50b)

Moreover, if either

\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
or

\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
holds, then

\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}
.

Applying the proposition

n^{-3/2}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}-\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t=o_{n}(1)O(|b-a|)
to Eq. (
49
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{kl,i}^{n}}[\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}]=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\big[[\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})]^{-1}+o_{n}(1)\big]\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)O(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\Big)

(51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\Big[[\lambda_{k}p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})]^{-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{kl}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)+o_{n}(1)O(\varepsilon)\Big],

since
\sup_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})=O(\varepsilon)
and

\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}\|\leq\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}\|<\infty
by
Proposition
1
.

To rewrite
\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s
in terms of the workload,
recall the key relation

\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}=\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\sum_{r}p_{r}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{rl}^{n}}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}+o_{n}(1)
given in Proposition
4
(see
Section
D.5
for its proof). We can
further approximate the queue length process
\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}
using the service rate

\widetilde{\mu}_{l}
and the remaining workload process
\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}
.

Lemma 15

(Relation between
\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}
)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose Assumptions
1
,
2
,
and
3
hold. Then, for p-FCFS policies

\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}-\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow 0
for all
l\in[K]
.

See Section
D.6
for the
proof. Applying Proposition
4
and
Lemma
15
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s=

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\frac{p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}}{\sum_{r}p_{r}\widetilde{q}_{rl}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)O(|b-a|).

Plugging this into the expression (
51
) for

\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{kl,i}^{n}}[\tau_{l}^{n}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi_{kl,i}^{n}}[\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}]=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\Big[[\lambda_{k}p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})]^{-1}\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\frac{p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}}{\sum_{r}p_{r}\widetilde{q}_{rl}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)O(t_{i+1}-t_{i})\Big)+o_{n}(1)\Big]

(52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\Big[[\widetilde{\rho}_{l}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})]^{-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s+o_{n}(1)\Big],

where we use the shorthands
\widetilde{p}_{l}=\sum_{r}p_{r}\widetilde{q}_{rl}
and

\widetilde{\rho}_{l}=\frac{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}}{\widetilde{\mu}_{l}}
in the final line.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
14
(Gap A2)

For any
l\in[K]
, Assumption
4
implies
\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
is continuous and strictly
increasing. Hence,

g(x):=x+\sum_{l=2}^{K}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\cdot(\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{1}}{\widetilde{\mu}_{l}}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}^{-1}x)\Big)

(53)

is continuous and strictly increasing with
g(0)=0
,
g(r)\geq r
. Let

x_{1}(r)
be a unique solution to
g(x)=r
and

x_{l}(r):=\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\cdot(\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{1}}{\widetilde{\mu}_{l}}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{\rho}_{1}^{-1}x_{1})\Big),~\forall~l\geq 2.

Evidently,
\underline{h}(r)=(x_{1}(r),\ldots,x_{K}(r))
is a unique solution
to Eq. (
47
). To see (ii), note that
x_{1}(r)
is
continuous with
x_{1}(0)=0
since
g^{-1}
is continuous.
For (iii), for
r=0
,
\underline{h}(0)=\mathbf{0}
is clearly an optimal
solution to
\text{Opt}(0)
. When
r>0
, we verify
\underline{h}(r)
satisfies
the KKT conditions for
\text{Opt}(r)
. This is evident from the fact that

\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}(0)=(\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}(0)=0
and
\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
and
(\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l})^{-1}
are strictly
increasing (Assumption
4
).

D.4
Proof of Proposition
5

Our proof for Eqs. (
50a
) and (
50b
) is similar to the proof for
Van Mieghem [66, Proposition 4]
. To
show Eqs. (
50a
), consider the cumulative cost during

t\in[a,b]
where each job incurs a unit cost per unit time spent in the
system. We study three different cost charging schemes

\displaystyle\text{Cost}_{1}^{n}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\sum_{i=\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(na)}^{\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(nb)}\widetilde{\tau}^{n}_{li},

\displaystyle\text{Cost}_{2}^{n}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{kl}(t)\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\text{Cost}_{3}^{n}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\sum_{i=\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(na)}^{\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(nb)-\widetilde{N}^{n}_{kl}(nb)}\widetilde{\tau}^{n}_{li}.

\text{Cost}_{1}^{n}(a,b)
charges the entire cost at the job’s arrival,

\text{Cost}_{3}^{n}(a,b)
at the job’s departure, and
\text{Cost}_{2}^{n}(a,b)

continuously. It is easy to verify

\text{Cost}_{3}^{n}(a,b)\leq\text{Cost}_{2}^{n}(a,b)\leq\text{Cost}_{1}^{n}(a,b),

and

\displaystyle n^{-3/2}(\text{Cost}_{1}^{n}(a,b)-\text{Cost}_{3}^{n}(a,b))=

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\sum_{i=\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(nb)-\widetilde{N}^{n}_{kl}(nb)+1}^{\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(nb)}\widetilde{\tau}^{n}_{li}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-1/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{kl}\|\|\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\|\rightarrow 0,

since
\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}\rightarrow\lambda p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}
by Assumption
2
, and
\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{kl}\|
and
\|\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\|
are bounded
(Proposition
1
). Conclude

\displaystyle o_{n}(1)=

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}-\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{N}_{kl}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

The proof for Eq. (
50b
) can be established similarly
and we omit the details.

For the second part of the proposition, recall from Proposition
1
(ii) that for
all
l\in[K]
,
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
and
\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
are equivalent. Recall that by Eq. (
50b
),

\frac{n^{-3/2}}{\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}_{l}^{n}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}(t)\mathrm{d}t=o_{n}(1),~\forall~l\in[K].

For simplicity, for fixed
[a,b]
, let
\xi^{n}_{l}
be the Lebesgue-Stieltjes
measure on
[0,1]
induced by
n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(n\cdot)
and
\xi_{l}
be the
Lebesgue-Stieltjes measure on
[0,1]
induced by
\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}e
. It is
easy to verify

n^{-3/2}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}^{n}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(t)=\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}~\mathrm{d}\xi^{n}_{l}.

Since
\xi^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\xi_{l}
on sets and

\|\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\|\leq\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\|<+\infty
eventually
(Proposition
1
), generalized Lebesgue convergence
[
53
, Page 270]

implies

n^{-3/2}\int_{na}^{nb}\widetilde{\tau}^{n}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(t)\rightarrow\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}(\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}e)(t)=\int_{a}^{b}\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

(54)

Next, we analyze the second term of Eq. (
50b
). Dominated convergence gives

\frac{1}{\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}t\rightarrow\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

(55)

Combining Eqs. (
50b
), (
54
), and (
55
) yields that for all
[a,b]\subset[0,1]
,

\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\lambda p_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}(b-a)}\int_{a}^{b}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

(56)

For a fixed
t\in[0,1]
, inserting
a=t,b=t+\Delta t
into Eq. (
56
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}(t+\Delta t)-\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}t}\int_{t}^{t+\Delta t}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}}\cdot\frac{1}{\Delta t}\int_{t}^{t+\Delta t}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s\rightarrow\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}(t),

(57)

as
\Delta t\rightarrow 0
, where the convergence follows from continuity of
\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}
by Eqs. (
35
) and (
39
), and the mean value theorem for definite integrals.
Similarly, one can show as
\Delta t\rightarrow 0
,

\frac{1}{\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}(t+\Delta t)-\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}t}\int_{t}^{t+\Delta t}\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}(s)\mathrm{d}s\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}(t).

(58)

Combining Eqs. (
56
), (
57
),
and (
58
) yields the desired result

\lambda\widetilde{p}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l},~\forall~l\in[K]
.

D.5
Proof of Proposition
4

Recalling the definition (
29
), for any
nt\in[0,n]

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{kl}^{n}(nt)=\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt)-\sum_{i=1}^{(\widetilde{M}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)}Y_{ik}^{n}\widetilde{Y}_{il}^{n}=\widetilde{A}_{kl}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{Z}^{n}_{kl}\Big((\widetilde{M}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)\Big).

By Lemma
4
, Proposition
3
, and Eq. (
36
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}_{kl}^{n}(nt)

\displaystyle=np_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}t+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(t)+o(n^{1/2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}(nt)

\displaystyle=n\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}t+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(t)+o(n^{1/2}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n}(nt)

\displaystyle=n\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)+o(n^{1/2}).

Recalling
\|\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}\|<+\infty
by Proposition
1
,
(\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)
can be reformulated as

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n}(nt)+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{S}}^{n}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n}(nt))+o(n^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}^{n}_{l}t+n^{1/2}\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}(t)+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{S}}^{n}_{l}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}^{n}_{l}(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}t+n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o(n^{-1/2}))+o(n^{1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}^{n}_{l}t+n^{1/2}\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}(t)+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{S}}^{n}_{l}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}^{n}_{l}(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}t+o_{n}(1))+o(n^{1/2}).

Therefore, we can rewrite
(\widetilde{M}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)
as

\displaystyle(\widetilde{M}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{S}_{l}^{n}\circ\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n})(nt)=

\displaystyle n\lambda^{n}t+n^{1/2}(\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n})^{-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)+n^{1/2}(\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t+o(1))

(59)

\displaystyle+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{M}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}t+o_{n}(1))+o(n^{1/2}).

Since
\widetilde{A}^{n}_{kl}(nt)=\lambda^{n}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}^{n}_{kl}nt+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{kl}^{n}(t)+o_{n}(1)
and
\widetilde{Z}_{kl}^{n}(nt)=p^{n}_{k}\widetilde{q}^{n}_{kl}nt+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(t)+o_{n}(1)
by the proof of Proposition
3
and
Definition
10
, combining
equations above yields

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)=

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{kl}^{n}(t)-\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\Big[\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)+\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t+o_{n}(1))\Big]

\displaystyle-p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{M}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}t+o_{n}(1))-\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}t+o_{n}(1))+o_{n}(1).

Moreover, the proof of Proposition
3
implies

\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l},\quad\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{kl}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{kl}:=\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}\circ\lambda e+p_{k}\widetilde{q}_{kl}\bm{A}_{0},\quad\bm{\widetilde{M}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{M}}_{l}:=-\widetilde{p}_{l}^{-1}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}\Big)\circ\widetilde{p}_{l}^{-1}e.

Since the limiting processes are continuous, by continuity of
composition
[
71
, Theorem 13.2.1]
and continuous mapping theorem, we
have that

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t+o_{n}(1))=\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t)+o_{n}(1),\quad\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{kl}^{n}-\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}e+o_{n}(1))=p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{0}^{n}+o_{n}(1),

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{M}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}\cdot+o_{n}(1))=-(\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n})^{-1}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\cdot)\Big)+o_{n}(1).

Thus, we can further rewrite
\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)
as

\displaystyle p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{0}^{n}(t)-\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\Big[\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)+\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t)\Big]+\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}t)+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}t)}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}-\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\Big[\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)+\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t)\Big]+\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}_{kl}^{n}(\lambda^{n}t)+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\left[\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}^{n}(\lambda^{n}\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t)\right]+o_{n}(1),

where the second line follows from the identity

\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}^{n}(t)=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{K}\bm{\widetilde{Z}}^{n}_{kl}(\lambda^{n}t)+\widetilde{p}^{n}_{l}\bm{A}^{n}_{0}(t)+o_{n}(1)
we derived in the proof of Proposition
3
. Then,
by (
37
), we have the desired result

\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}(t)=\frac{p_{k}^{n}\widetilde{q}_{kl}^{n}}{\widetilde{p}_{l}^{n}}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}(t)+o_{n}(1)
.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
15
(Gap A1)

For any
nt\in[0,n]
, let
\widetilde{v}^{n}_{l}(nt)
be the amount of service, if any,
already given to the oldest predicted class
l
job present in the system at
time
nt
. By definition,

t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}(t)=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\big[\widetilde{V}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt))-\widetilde{V}_{l}^{n}\big(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)\big)-\widetilde{v}^{n}_{l}(nt)\big]

(60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\big[(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt))-(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}\big(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-n^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)\big)\big]

\displaystyle+\big[\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}^{n}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt))-\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}^{n}\big(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-n^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)\big)\big]+o_{n}(1)-n^{-1/2}\widetilde{v}^{n}_{l}(nt),

where the second equality follows from Proposition
3
, and
o_{n}(\cdot)
is uniform over

t\in[0,1]
. Under the predicted-class GI/GI/1 structure,

\underline{\bar{V}}^{n}_{l}(s)=s/\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}
is linear, so the first term in (
60
) collapses to

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\big[(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt))-(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}\big(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-n^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)\big)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~n^{-1/2}(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)=(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t).

Since
\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\to\widetilde{\mu}_{l}
(Assumption
2
) and

\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}\|<+\infty
(Proposition
1
),

(\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)=(\widetilde{\mu}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(1)

uniformly in
t
. The collapse-first ordering means only convergence

\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\to\widetilde{\mu}_{l}
(no rate) is used in this step.

It remains to bound the second term in Eq. (
60
). Notice that since
\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}^{n}=\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}+o_{n}(1)
where
\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}
is
uniformly continuous on compact intervals by Proposition
3
and

\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}\|<+\infty
by Proposition
1
,

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}^{n}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt))-\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}^{n}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-n^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt))-\bm{\widetilde{V}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-n^{-1}\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt))+o_{n}(1)=o_{n}(1).

D.7
Complementary proof for Proposition 6 in
Van Mieghem [66]
(Gap A3)

Compared to the proof of
Van Mieghem [66, Proposition 6]
, we adopt a
different partition of the time interval
[0,1]
to derive Eq. (
46
) using the mean-value theorem. To show an
analogous result
[
66
, Eq. (94)]
, Van Mieghem picks a partition
using stopping times of
\bm{W}_{+}
to ensure sufficiently small variation of

\bm{W}_{+}
over each subinterval. Without justification,
Van Mieghem [66]

claims the partition size is small enough (
O(\varepsilon)
).

Despite best efforts, we found proving this claim difficult when the workload

\bm{W}_{+}
is a general reflected process. When
\bm{W}_{+}
is a reflected
Brownian motion, we give a proof that the partition size is still

\sup_{i}(t_{i+1}-t_{i})=O(\varepsilon)
in Lemma
16
below; hence (
46
) would follow
even if
\{t_{i}\}
is chosen as the stopping times as in
Van Mieghem [66]
.
Our proof exploits the almost sure non-differentiablity of sample paths of
reflected Brownian motions. (Alternatively, our previous proof provides a
simple justification for
[
66
, Eq. (94)]
using our mean value
theorem result (
46
).)

Lemma 16

(Stopping times of
\bm{W}_{+}
)
.

Given
\varepsilon>0
, consider the sequence of stopping times

\{t_{i}(\varepsilon):i\in\mathbb{N}\}
of
\bm{W}_{+}

\displaystyle t_{1}(\varepsilon)=\min\{1,\inf\{0<t\leq 1:|\bm{W}_{+}(t)-\lfloor\bm{W}_{+}(0)/\varepsilon\rfloor\varepsilon|\geq\varepsilon\}\},

\displaystyle t_{i+1}(\varepsilon)=\min\{1,\inf\{t_{i}(\varepsilon)<t\leq 1:|\bm{W}_{+}(t)-\bm{W}_{+}(t_{i}(\varepsilon))|\geq\varepsilon\}\}.

Then, we have that

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\sup_{i}(t_{i+1}(\varepsilon)-t_{i}(\varepsilon))=0

Proof.

We prove by contradiction and will show that if Lemma
16
does not hold, then there exists
[a,b]\subset[0,1]

such that
b-a>0
and
\bm{W}_{+}
is a constant on
[a,b]
. We argue that
the latter leads to a contraction using that
\bm{W}_{+}
is a reflected
Brownian motion as shown in Proposition
1
. If
\bm{W}_{+}(t)=0
for

t\in[a,b]
, then the underlying Brownian motion must stay at its running
minimum throughout
[a,b]
because of the definition of the reflection
mapping
[
71
]
, but this is a zero probability
event
[
45
]
. If
\bm{W}_{+}(t)=c
for some positive constant
c

and
t\in[a,b]
, it is contradictory to the nondifferentiability of
Brownian motion
[
45
]
.

Suppose for the purpose of contradiction that there exists some

\delta>0
, a sequence of
\varepsilon_{k}\rightarrow 0
, and a sequence of

\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
satisfying

\displaystyle t_{i_{k}+1}(\varepsilon_{k})-t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k})\geq\delta,\text{ and }|\bm{W}_{+}(t)-\bm{W}_{+}(t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}))|\leq\varepsilon_{k},~\forall~t\in[t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}),t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k})+\delta]\subset[0,1].

Let

I(k)=[t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}),t_{i_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k})+\delta]\subset[0,1]
for all
k\geq 1
. We claim that there exists
b-a\geq\delta_{0}>0

and a subsequence
\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
such that
[a,b]\subset I(k_{l})

for all
l\geq 1
. Let
M=\lceil 2/\delta\rceil
. Partition
[0,1]

into

0=a_{0}<a_{1}<\cdots<a_{M}=1

with

a_{r+1}-a_{r}=\delta/2>0
, possibly except the last
interval. Evidently, there exists some
r_{0}\in\{0,1,...,M-1\}
such
that
[a_{r_{0}},a_{r_{0}+1}]\cap I(k)\neq\emptyset,
for infinitely many

k
’s; otherwise

\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\#\{k:[a_{m},a_{m+1}]\cap I(k)\neq\emptyset,k\in\mathbb{N}\}<+\infty
, so that

\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\#\{k:[a_{m},a_{m+1}]\cap I(k)\neq\emptyset,k\in\mathbb{N}\}\geq\#\{k:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}=\infty
gives a
contradiction.

We next construct the aforementioned interval
[a,b]
and subsequence
\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
.
Since
[a_{r_{0}},a_{r_{0}+1}]\cap I(k)\neq\emptyset
for infinitely many
k
, at least one of the following statement hold:

(i)

there exists a subsequence
\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})>a_{r_{0}}
for all
l
;

(ii)

there exists a subsequence
\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})+\delta<a_{r_{0}+1}
for all
l
;

(iii)

there exists a subsequence
\{k_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})\leq a_{r_{0}}<a_{r_{0}+1}\leq t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})+\delta
for all
l
.

For the case of (i), by definition we have that for all
l
,
a_{r_{0}}<t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})\leq a_{r_{0}+1}
since
I(k_{l})\cap[a_{r_{0}},a_{r_{0}+1}]\neq\emptyset
. Therefore, for all
l
, we have that
a_{r_{0}}+\delta<t_{i_{k_{l}}}(\varepsilon_{k_{l}})+\delta\leq a_{r_{0}+1}+\delta
. In other words,

[a_{r_{0}+1},a_{r_{0}}+\delta]\subset I_{k_{l}},~\forall~l\geq 1
.
Hence, we can set
a=a_{r_{0}+1},b=a_{r_{0}}+\delta
, where
b-a\geq\delta/2
. For (ii) and (iii),
we can construct
a
and
b
similarly and we skip the details here.

Then, by
[a,b]\subset I(k_{l})
, we have that

\sup_{a\leq t,t^{\prime}\leq b}|\bm{W}_{+}(t)-\bm{W}_{+}(t^{\prime})|\leq 2\varepsilon_{k_{l}},~\forall~l\geq 1
,
which implies that
\bm{W}_{+}(t)
is a constant on
[a,b]
. This completes our proof.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of the heavy traffic optimality of P
c\mu
-rule (Theorem
2
)

E.1
Overview of the proof

Our goal is to show convergence of
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}
to a limit satisfying (
13
) under P
c\mu
-rule, from which
Lemma
3
will imply Theorem
2
.

Relationships between
(\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}^{n})
and
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}

Since the P
c\mu
-rule uses observable
ages
, we need to connect
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}

and the endogenous processes

(\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n},\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}^{n}),~\forall~l\in[K]
. We prove the
equivalence between the original KKT conditions (
13
) and the modified version for age (
14
),
provided that either
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}
or

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}
. For predicted class
l\in[K]
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}
is the limiting arrival rate (see Definition
10
).

Proposition 6

(Relationship between
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}
)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
and a sequence of queueing systems, suppose that
Assumptions
1
,

2
, and
3
hold. Under p-FCFS feasible policies, for any
predicted class
l\in[K]
, (i)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}-\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow{0}
, (ii)

\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\|<\infty
; (iii)
\{\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\}_{n}
converges
iff
\{\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n}\}_{n}
converges; (iv) their limits coincide: if there exist

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l},\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}

and
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}
, then
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}
.

The proof of Proposition
6
requires the arrival rates of the predicted classes to converge to
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}\}_{l\in[K]}
, which is implied by Assumption
2
. We also characterize the relationship between

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}
and the policy process
\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}
in Corollary
20
, which allows for directly analyzing the dynamics of the scaled age
process
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}
.

Convergence of the P
c\mu
indices and the scaled age processes

Since the P
c\mu
-rule serves the job that has the highest index value, the gap
between the class indices becomes small and the convergence (
14
) holds.

Proposition 7

(Convergence of max difference of the P
c\mu
indices)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
,

4
, and
5
hold. Under the P
c\mu
-rule,

\sup\limits_{t\in[0,1]}\,\max\limits_{l,m\in[K]}\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t)\big|\rightarrow{0}.

(61)

By the continuity of the inverse cost function
(\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}
,
convergence of
\{\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\}_{l\in[K]}
follows (Lemma
17
) and we will have the desired final result.

We prove Proposition
7
in
Section
F
.
Specifically, we partition
[0,1]
, the domain of the diffusion-scaled
processes, into intervals of size
O(n^{-1/2})
and show that

\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t)|
do not exhibit
substantial growth within each interval given the carefully chosen size of the interval. The main technical challenge is to demonstrate that such
growth does not accumulate over time. Since

\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(0)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(0)|=0
, we proceed via
induction over the intervals within the partition: for a fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we show that

(i)

at each endpoint
t
of every interval,
\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t)|\leq\varepsilon
(Propositions
8
and
9
);

(ii)

within each interval
I
,
\sup_{t\in I}\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t)|\leq 3\varepsilon/2
(Proposition
10
).

We outline the proof for part (i) (part (ii) can be shown similarly).
Given an interval
[t_{1},t_{2}]
and an induction hypothesis assumed for
t_{1}
, by symmetry it suffices to show

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t_{2})\leq\varepsilon

for any
l,m\in[K]
.
First, for the case that predicted class
m
is selected
by the P
c\mu
-rule at some time
ns\in[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
,
we use definition of the P
c\mu
-rule to bound growth of
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t_{2})
. In particular,

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t_{2})\leq\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(s)]}_{\text{bounded increase, }\leq\varepsilon/2}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(s)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(s)]}_{\text{by the P$c\mu$-rule, $\leq 0$}}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(s)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{m}(t_{2})]}_{\text{bounded increase, }\leq\varepsilon/2},

(62)

where the first and the last term are bounded by
\varepsilon/2
due to
our choice of
t_{2}-t_{1}=O(n^{-1/2})
and the smoothness of the cost
functions in Assumption
4
, and the second term is non-positive since predicted class
m
is chosen by the P
c\mu
-rule at time
ns
.

For the other case that predicted class
m
is never selected
by the P
c\mu
-rule during the interval
[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
, the analysis is
more involved and requires development of novel analysis techniques.
If the server is idling at some
ns\in[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
, our analysis
is similar to (
62
) and the second term becomes zero since the P
c\mu
-rule is work-conserving. Otherwise, if there is no idling during
[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
, then intuitively, the server is busy serving other
K-1
predicted classes.
By heavy traffic assumption
\sum_{l}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}=1

(Assumption
2
), there exists at least one
predicted class
k_{0}^{n}
that receives sufficient service from the server
(See (
72
)) and incurs sufficient descent
in the age process (Lemma
24
) in
[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
.
Then, by strong convexity of the
Pc
\mu
cost
\widetilde{C}_{k_{0}^{n}}
(Assumption
5
), the
Pc
\mu
index of class
k^{n}_{0}
, say
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}
,
also incurs sufficient descent (Lemma
25
). Such
descent in the
Pc
\mu
index enables us to bound the growth of
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}

and derive the desired result in Proposition
9
.

E.2
Comparison to the optimality result in
Van Mieghem [66]

Plugging
Q^{n}=I
, our proof gives the optimality of the well-known G
c\mu
-rule where true class labels are known
[
66
]
. In this special case,
our analysis identifies missing arguments in
Van Mieghem [66]
’s original
proof and provides conditions under which their original claims hold. For
example, the derivatives of the first-order approximations for arrival time partial sum processes,
\{(\widetilde{\lambda}^{n}_{l})^{-1}e\}_{l\in[K]}
, converge to
\{(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l})^{-1}e\}_{l\in[K]}
in our framework. We find the same condition on the analogous processes,
\{\bar{U}_{k}^{n}\}_{k\in[K]}
in
Van Mieghem [66]
, to be one of the two alternative conditions that suffice to correctly connect the age and sojourn time processes in the G/G/1 systems. We refer the reader to Section
G
for detailed explanations.

The first missing piece is that the G
c\mu
-rule uses the ages of waiting jobs for
scheduling, but
Van Mieghem [66]
does not prove the G
c\mu
-rule achieves
optimality conditions defined in terms of the sojourn times
[
66
, Eq
(54)]
. In Section
G
, using our proof for Proposition
6
, we provide sufficient conditions to establish the equivalence between convergence of the scaled age and sojourn time processes in that paper, and complement the proof for the optimality of the G
c\mu
-rule.

To prove the optimality of the G
c\mu
-rule rigorously, our analysis also shows that the age processes converge to a limit satisfying the optimality condition under the G
c\mu
-rule using
Proposition
7
with
Q^{n}=I
, and thus
condition (
13
) (extension of
Van Mieghem [66, Eq
(54)]
) is satisfied. This missing justification was nontrivial
(to us), and we hope our rigorous arguments provide analytical value to
subsequent works.

We found the proof of Proposition
7
to be novel and involved. Our analysis of the index dynamics with the
particular choice of the partition size of the time horizon entails carefully handling errors of
diffusion approximations for predicted classes (Proposition
1
and
3
). We control the
evolution of
\{\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\}_{l\in[K]}
under the P
c\mu
-rule, which requires
formally establishing relationships between
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}
,
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n}
, and

\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}^{n}
.

In particular, our proof of Proposition
7
identifies a previously unstated sufficient condition: strong convexity of the cost functions in Assumption
5
. The curvature ensures that if some
predicted class is not served and its index increases in a subinterval of the
partition, then there is another predicted class
k^{n}_{0}
that receives ample
service so that the index
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}
decreases enough
(Lemma
25
), implying that
the gap between the indices remains small. On the other hand, under the

strict
convexity
Van Mieghem [66]
assumes, we were unable to show
the desired convergence he claims (either
[
66
, Eq (54)]
or a
more general version in Proposition
7
).

E.3
Comparison to the optimality result in
Mandelbaum and Stolyar [40]

Our analysis with perfect classification (
Q^{n}=I
) also identifies missing pieces in the optimality proof by
Mandelbaum and Stolyar [40]
for the G
c\mu
-rule with sojourn time cost (called
D\text{-}Gc\mu
in
[
40
]
) in single-server systems and provides conditions for the claims to hold.

First, similarly to
[
66
]
,
Mandelbaum and Stolyar [40]
suggests
using age processes for the
D\text{-}Gc\mu
rule, but they did not prove that their

D\text{-}Gc\mu
rule satisfies optimality conditions they adopted, which are based
on sojourn time processes and identical to (
13
) in
the single-server case.
Specifically, we find that
[
40
, Eq (66)]
that connects
D\text{-}Gc\mu
to the preceding analysis in
[
40
]
should have been shown in terms of the queue length and age processes similarly to our Proposition
6
(i). Using an equivalence between the age and sojourn time processes analogous to Proposition
6
(iii) and (iv), the optimality of
D\text{-}Gc\mu
could be obtained.

Our analysis shows the optimality of the
D\text{-}Gc\mu
in the single-server case
requires weaker assumptions on cost functions than those adopted
in
Mandelbaum and Stolyar [40]
. The optimality in
[
40
, Theorem 2]
is built on the attraction propery of the fluid-scaled queue length limit
[
40
, Theorem 3]
. In the single-server case, the key implications of the attraction property are the small gaps between the class indices over subintervals
[
40
, Eqs. (55), (56)]
, which are analogous to our Propositions
8
,
9
, and
10
.
Mandelbaum and Stolyar [40, Theorem 3]
requires the cost functions to be twice continuously differentiable (and strongly convex) in order for the workload and queue length limits to be amenable to analysis in the multi-server setting. In contrast, our analysis directly identifies the dynamics of the age processes under the
D\text{-}Gc\mu
in the single-server case, and prove the counterpart propositions under the weaker conditions, namely Assumptions
4
and
5
.

E.4
Detailed proof of Theorem
2

We begin by showing the convergence of the age process, whose proof we give in
Section
E.5
. We write

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}:=\{\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\}_{l\in[K]}
for the vector-valued diffusion-scaled age process.

Lemma 17

(Convergence of
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}
)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5
hold. Under the P
c\mu
-rule, there exists
\bm{\widetilde{a}}\in\mathcal{C}^{K}
such that

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{a}}
in
(\mathcal{D}^{K},\|\cdot\|)

\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}
-a.s..

From the above lemma and Proposition
1
, the relation between

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}^{n}
in Proposition
6
implies convergence of
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}
,

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}
,
\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}
,

\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}
, and
\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}
.

If
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l},\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
, Proposition
6
implies
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}
. Since

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
by Lemma
17
, it is easy
to verify
\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l},\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l},\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
from the relation between

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}
in Proposition
6
, relation (
37
) between
\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}
and

\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}
, and relation (
33
) between
\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}
and

\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}
. Using the relation (
38
) between

\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}
,
\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}
, and
\bm{\widetilde{T}}_{l}
, one can check that

\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}\in\mathcal{C}
.

By Lemma
15
and Proposition
5
, we have
\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}/\widetilde{\rho}_{l},~\forall~l\in[K]
.
Proposition
7
then implies

\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}/\widetilde{\rho}_{l},~\forall~l\in[K],\quad\sum_{l\in[K]}\bm{\widetilde{W}}_{l}=\bm{W}_{+},\quad\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l})=\widetilde{\mu}_{m}\widetilde{C}_{m}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{m}),~\forall~l,m\in[K].

(63)

By Lemma
14
, it follows

\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{\tau}}_{l}=[h(\bm{W}_{+})]_{l},~\forall~l\in[K]
. This yields

\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(\cdot;Q^{n})\rightarrow\bm{J}^{*}(\cdot;Q)

\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}\text{-}a.s.

according to Theorem
1
and
Lemma
3
.

The weak convergence on the original systems
\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(\cdot;Q^{n})\Rightarrow\bm{J}^{*}(\cdot;Q)
in

(\mathcal{D},\|\cdot\|)
follows from
[
31
, Lemma 3.2, Lemma 3.7]
.
Moreover, for any
x\in\mathbb{R},~t\in[0,1],
by reverse Fatou’s lemma and the
\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}\text{-}a.s.
convergence of
\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(\cdot;Q^{n})
, we have

\displaystyle\lim\sup_{n}\mathbb{P}^{n}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(t;Q^{n})>x]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}}[\lim\sup_{n}\mathbb{I}\{\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(t;Q^{n})>x\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}}[\mathbb{I}\{\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q^{n})>x\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q)>x].

Combining this with
\lim\inf_{n}\mathbb{P}^{n}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(t;Q^{n})>x]\geq\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q)>x]
from Theorem
1
gives the desired result:

\mathbb{P}^{n}[\bm{J}^{n}_{\text{Pc$\mu$}}(t;Q^{n})>x]\rightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\text{copy}}[\bm{J}^{*}(t;Q)>x]
.

E.5
Proof of Lemma
17

By Proposition
7
and
Lemma
21
, the P
c\mu
-rule gives

\max_{l,s\in[K]}\|\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l})-\widetilde{\mu}_{s}\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{s})\|\rightarrow 0
. Given
s\in[K]
, for any
l\in[K]
, since
\widetilde{\mu}_{l}>0
by
Assumption
1
and Definition
10
, we have

\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\tilde{a}_{l}^{n})-\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{s}}{\widetilde{\mu}_{l}}\widetilde{C}_{s}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n})\rightarrow 0.
Letting

f_{s}(\cdot):=\sum_{l=1}^{K}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\cdot(\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}\big(\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{s}}{\widetilde{\mu}_{l}}\widetilde{C}_{s}^{\prime}(\cdot)\big)
, note that

\sum_{l=1}^{K}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}-\big(f_{s}\circ\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n}\big)\rightarrow 0

from continuity of
(\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime})^{-1}
(Assumption
4
). Under p-FCFS feasible policies,
Lemma
15
and Proposition
6
implies

\bm{\widetilde{W}}^{n}_{l}-\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow 0
. Applying Proposition
1
, there
exists
\bm{W}_{+}\in\mathcal{C}([0,1],\mathbb{R})
such that

\sum_{l=1}^{K}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{W}_{+}
. Hence,

f_{s}\circ\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n}\rightarrow\bm{W}_{+}
.

Since
f_{s}
is continuous and strictly increasing,
f_{s}^{-1}
is
well-defined and also continuous. Conclude

(f_{s}^{-1},f_{s}\circ\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n})\rightarrow(f_{s}^{-1},\bm{W}_{+})
in

\mathcal{C}^{2}
under the product topology induced by
\|\cdot\|
. By
the continuity of composition (e.g.,
[
71
, Theorem 13.2.1]
),

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n}=f_{s}^{-1}\circ\big(f_{s}\circ\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}^{n}\big)\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}:=f_{s}^{-1}\circ\bm{W}_{+}
where
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{s}\in\mathcal{C}
by continuity of

f_{s}^{-1}
and
\bm{W}_{+}
. This completes our proof.

E.6
Proof of Proposition
6
(Gap B2)

We show the asymptotically linear relationship (i) between
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}
and

\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}
. Other results immediately follow from (i) and
Propositions
1
and Proposition
5
. We use a
reformulation of the age process.

Claim 2
.

\widetilde{a}_{l}^{n}(nt)=nt-\widetilde{U}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1)+o_{n}(n^{1/2}).

(64)

Since

\widetilde{U}^{n}_{l}(nt)=n(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{n})^{-1}t+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{U}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{1/2})
by
Proposition
3
, we can
further rewrite (
64
) as

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t)=n^{1/2}[t-(\widetilde{\lambda}^{n}_{l})^{-1}(n^{-1}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1))]-\bm{\widetilde{U}}^{n}_{l}(n^{-1}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1))+o_{n}(1).

Recall
\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt)=n\widetilde{\lambda}^{n}_{l}t+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{1/2})
by
Proposition
3
,

\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}:=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{N}_{kl}^{n}
by Definition
11
, and

\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{kl}^{n}\|\leq\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}\|<+\infty
by
Proposition
1
. Evidently,

\displaystyle n^{-1}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1)=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}^{n}_{l}t+n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)-n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}t+n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)-n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{-1/2})=\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}t+o_{n}(1),

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{U}}^{n}_{l}(n^{-1}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{l}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}t+o_{n}(1))+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t+o_{n}(1))+o_{n}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(1)

where we used
n^{1/2}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{n}|=o_{n}(1)
by Assumption
2
and Proposition
3
in step (a),
\bm{\widetilde{U}}^{n}_{l}\rightarrow\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{l}
in step (b) and
\bm{\widetilde{U}}_{l}(t)=-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l})^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{-1}t)
in step (c) by the proof of Proposition
3
, and the uniform continuity of
\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}
on compact intervals in step (d).
Using Proposition
3
, as well as the right-hand side of step (a), we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}[t-(\widetilde{\lambda}^{n}_{l})^{-1}(n^{-1}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}t-n^{1/2}(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{n})^{-1}\big(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}t+n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}^{n}(t)-n^{-1/2}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}(t)+o(n^{-1/2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\widetilde{\lambda}_{l})^{-1}[\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)-\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)]+o_{n}(1).

Proof of claim

For fixed
t\in[0,1]
, we first consider the case that
\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt)=0
. Since there is no arrival to the predicted class
l
at time
nt
, it is
easy to verify that
\widetilde{a}^{n}_{l}(nt)=0
,
\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)=0
, and

nt\leq\widetilde{u}_{l1}
. Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big|\widetilde{a}_{l}^{n}(nt)-[nt-\widetilde{U}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1)]\Big|=|0-[nt-\widetilde{U}_{l}^{n}(1)]|\leq

\displaystyle|\widetilde{u}_{l1}|=o_{n}(n^{1/2}),

where the last equality follows from Propositon
3
. When
\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)\geq 1
,

\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)
jobs from the predicted class
l
have completed
service and exited the queue. Under a p-FCFS policy, the oldest customer from
the predicted class
l
at time
nt
corresponds to the

[\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1]
th arrival of predicted class
l
. From the
definition of
\widetilde{a}^{n}_{l}(nt)
as the time difference between
nt
and the
arrival time of the oldest job in predicted class
l
, the exact formulation

\widetilde{a}^{n}_{l}(nt)=nt-\widetilde{U}_{l}^{n}(\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(nt)-\widetilde{N}_{l}^{n}(nt)+1)
follows. This
completes our proof of (
64
).

Appendix F
Proof for convergence of the P
c\mu
indices (Proposition
7
, Gap B1)

Recalling the strong convexity of
\widetilde{C}_{l}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}_{l}(y)\geq\widetilde{C}_{l}(x)+\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(x)(y-x)+\frac{m}{2}(y-x)^{2},~\forall~x,y,~\forall~l\in[K].

for some
m>0
, we use the following constants

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{\min}=\min_{l\in[K]}\widetilde{\mu}_{l},\quad\widetilde{\mu}_{\max}=\max_{l\in[K]}\widetilde{\mu}_{l},\quad\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}=\min_{l\in[K]}\widetilde{\rho}_{l},\quad\widetilde{\rho}_{\max}=\max_{l\in[K]}\widetilde{\rho}_{l},

(65)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}:=\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}}{3(K-1)\widetilde{\rho}_{\max}},\quad\beta_{0}:=\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{\min}\alpha_{0}}{2},\quad\gamma_{0}:=\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}\alpha_{0}}{1-\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}}.

Since
\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}
is uniformly continuous on the compact set

[0,\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\|]
where
\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\|<+\infty

according to Proposition
6
, we
have the following result.

Lemma 18

(Continuity of
\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}
)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose that Assumption
4
holds. For any
\varepsilon>0
,
there exists
\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)>0
such that for
any
a_{1},a_{2}\in[0,\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\|]
,

(i)

if
|a_{2}-a_{1}|\leq\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
, then

|\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(a_{2})-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(a_{1})|<\frac{\varepsilon}{8\widetilde{\mu}_{\max}},~\forall~l\in[K]
;

(ii)

if
|a_{2}-a_{1}|\leq\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)
, then
|\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(a_{2})-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(a_{1})|<\frac{m\beta_{0}}{2\widetilde{\mu}_{\max}}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),~\forall~l\in[K]
.

Our proof is separated into three propositions. Below, we suppose
Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
,
5
,
and
3
hold. For any
\varepsilon>0
, let

\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)
be constants defined in
Lemma
18
and define

\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon):=n^{-1/2}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),\delta_{2}^{n}(\varepsilon):=n^{-1/2}\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)
. Partition the time
interval
[0,n]
into subintervals of length no more than

n\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
. Let
N(\varepsilon)
be large enough that the three
propositions below hold for
n\geq N(\varepsilon)
. We prove the proposition by
an inductive argument over these subintervals, given after the three
propositions are stated.

See Section
F.2
for the proof of the first proposition.

Proposition 8

(Max difference of the P
c\mu
indices at endpoints: Case I)
.

Let
t_{1}\in[0,1-\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)]
be such that

\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(t_{1})|<\varepsilon

and all predicted classes are selected by the P
c\mu
-rule in

[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))]
. Then, there exists

N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any
n>N(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\max_{l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))|<\varepsilon.

We prove the second proposition in Section
F.3
.

Proposition 9

(Max difference of the P
c\mu
-rule indices at endpoints: Case
II)
.

Let
t_{1}\in[0,1-\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)]
be such that

\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(t_{1})|<\varepsilon

and some predicted class is NOT selected for service under the P
c\mu
-rule in

[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon))]
. Then, there exists

N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any
n>N(\varepsilon)

(i)

(No Idling) if there is no server idle time in
[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))]
, then there exists

s^{n}_{1}\in[t_{1}+\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon),t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)]
such that

\max_{l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})|<\varepsilon
;

(ii)

(Idling) if server idling occurs in
[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))]
,
then
\max_{l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))|<\varepsilon.

Finally, see Section
F.4
for
the proof of the third proposition.

Proposition 10

(Max difference of the P
c\mu
-rule indices within intervals)
.

Let
t_{1}\in[0,1]
be such that

\max_{l,m\in[K]}\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(t_{1})\big|<\varepsilon
. Then, there exists
N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any

n>N(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\max_{l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]}\,\sup_{t\in[t_{1},(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1]}\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t)\big|<3\varepsilon/2.

Proof of Proposition
7
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
n\geq N(\varepsilon)
. Call a point
s\in[0,1]

\varepsilon
-good
if

\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(s)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(s)|<\varepsilon
. We construct an
increasing sequence of
\varepsilon
-good points
0=s_{0}<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{J}=1
whose
consecutive gaps satisfy

\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)\leq s_{j+1}-s_{j}\leq\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon),\qquad j=0,\dots,J-1.

(66)

Base case.
At
t=0
all ages are zero, so all indices coincide
and
s_{0}=0
is
\varepsilon
-good.

Recursive step.
Suppose
s_{j}<1
is
\varepsilon
-good. We obtain
the next
\varepsilon
-good point by applying the relevant proposition on

[s_{j},(s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1]
.

(a)

If all predicted classes are served on this interval, then
Proposition
8
makes

s_{j+1}:=(s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1

\varepsilon
-good.

(b)

If some predicted class is not served and either idling occurs or

s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)\geq 1
, then
Proposition
9
(ii) makes the same

s_{j+1}:=(s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1

\varepsilon
-good.

(c)

Otherwise some predicted class is not served and no idling occurs, and
Proposition
9
(i) provides an
\varepsilon
-good
point

s_{j+1}\in[s_{j}+\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon),\,s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)]
.

In every case the maximal index gap is restored to below
\varepsilon
at

s_{j+1}
, so the error does not accumulate across steps, and (
66
) holds because
\gamma_{0}\in(0,1)
. Each step advances time by at
least
\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)=\gamma_{0}n^{-1/2}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)>0
,
so the recursion reaches
s_{J}=1
after at most

\lceil n^{1/2}/(\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon))\rceil
steps.

From the good points to the uniform bound.
Each

\varepsilon
-good
s_{j}
meets the hypothesis of
Proposition
10
, which gives

\sup_{t\in[s_{j},(s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1]}\,\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(t)|<3\varepsilon/2.

Since
s_{j+1}\leq s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
, the intervals

[s_{j},(s_{j}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))\wedge 1]
cover
[0,1]
, hence

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{l,m\in[K]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{m}^{n}(t)|<3\varepsilon/2
for
all
n\geq N(\varepsilon)
. As
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, (
61
) follows.

F.1
Preliminaries

Facts about limiting diffusion processes

We use the following
basic facts to analyze the dynamics of
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}
.

Lemma 19

(Continuity of
\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}
)
.

There exists
N(\varepsilon)
such that for
n>N(\varepsilon)
and

t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
,

(i)

if
|t_{2}-t_{1}|<\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)
, then

|\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t_{1})|<\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)/3,~\forall~l\in[K]
;

(ii)

if
|t_{2}-t_{1}|<\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)
, then

|\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{2}))-\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1}))|<\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)/3,~\forall~l\in[K]
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3
, we
have
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t+o_{n}(1))-\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)|=o_{n}(1)
by uniform
continuity of
\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}
over a closed interval of which
[0,1]
is a proper
subset for all
l\in[K]
. (i) is a direct consequence of

|t_{2}-t_{1}|=o_{n}(1)
. To see (ii), we have

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(t+o_{n}(1))-\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(t)|=o_{n}(1)
similarly, and

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|n^{-1}\widetilde{T}_{l}(n(t+o_{n}(1)))-n^{-1}\widetilde{T}_{l}(nt)|=o_{n}(1)

by (
36
).
∎

Lemma 20

(Relation between
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}
and
\bm{\widetilde{T}}^{n}_{l}
)
.

Given a classifier
f_{\theta}
, suppose
Assumptions
1
,
2
, and
3
hold. Under
p-FCFS feasible policies,

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t)=n^{1/2}t-n^{-1/2}\widetilde{\rho}_{l}^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt)+\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)-\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))+o_{n}(1).

(67)

Proof.

Recalling
\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt)=n\underline{\bar{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{1/2})
,

\widetilde{S}^{n}_{l}(nt)=n\underline{\bar{S}}^{n}_{l}(t)+n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{S}}^{n}_{l}(t)+o_{n}(n^{1/2})

(Proposition
3
),

\displaystyle\bm{\widetilde{N}}^{n}_{l}(t)=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\underline{\bar{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)+\bm{\widetilde{A}}^{n}_{l}(t)-n^{1/2}\underline{\bar{S}}^{n}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))-\bm{\widetilde{S}}^{n}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))+o_{n}(1)

(68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\underline{\bar{A}}_{l}(t)+\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)-n^{1/2}\underline{\bar{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))-\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}t-n^{-1/2}\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt)+\bm{\widetilde{A}}_{l}(t)-\bm{\widetilde{S}}_{l}(n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))+o_{n}(1),

where we used
\widetilde{N}^{n}_{l}(nt)=\widetilde{A}^{n}_{l}(nt)-\widetilde{S}^{n}_{l}(\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt))
,

n^{1/2}(\underline{\bar{A}}^{n}_{l}-\underline{\bar{A}}_{l})=o_{n}(1)
,
n^{1/2}(\underline{\bar{S}}^{n}_{l}-\underline{\bar{S}}_{l})=o_{n}(1)

from Assumption
2
, and boundedness of

n^{-1}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(n\cdot)
(
36
). Noting

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t)=\widetilde{\lambda}_{l}^{-1}\bm{\widetilde{N}}_{l}^{n}(t)+o_{n}(1)
by
Proposition
6
, we have the desired result.
∎

Asymptotic P
c\mu
index

For any predicted class
l\in[K]
and
t\in[0,1]
, the P
c\mu
index and its
asymptotic counterpart is

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t):=\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\cdot n^{1/2}(\widetilde{C}^{n}_{l})^{\prime}(\widetilde{a}_{l}^{n}(nt)),~~~~~~\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t):=\widetilde{\mu}_{l}\cdot\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t))

(69)

Their difference can be bounded by

|\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)|\leq\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t))\cdot|\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}-\widetilde{\mu}_{l}|+\widetilde{\mu}^{n}_{l}\cdot|n^{1/2}(\widetilde{C}^{n}_{l})^{\prime}(n^{1/2}\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t))-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t))|.

Note that
\limsup_{n}\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}<+\infty
from

n^{1/2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{l}^{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{l})\rightarrow 0
(Assumption
2
),
\limsup_{n}\|\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(\cdot))\|<+\infty
since

\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
is continuous, and
\limsup_{n}\|\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}\|<+\infty
by
Proposition
6
. Since

n^{1/2}(\widetilde{C}^{n}_{l})^{\prime}(n^{1/2}\cdot)\rightarrow\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
by
Assumption
4
, we
can conclude
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)|=o_{n}(1)
.

Lemma 21
.

There exists
N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any

n\geq N(\varepsilon)
,

\max_{l\in[K]}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\big|\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)\big|\leq\min\Big\{\frac{\varepsilon}{16},\frac{m\beta_{0}}{4}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big\}.

Bounding the difference between P
c\mu
indices

When the difference of the scaled ages

\{\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}\}_{l\in[K]}
is bounded, we demonstrate bounded differences of
the indices over sufficiently small intervals.

Lemma 22

(P
c\mu
index: Continuity I)
.

There exists
N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any
n\geq N(\varepsilon)
,

l\in[K]
, and
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq 1
,

(i)

if
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t_{1})\leq\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
, then

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
;

(ii)

if
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t_{1})\geq 0
,
then
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})\geq\max\{-\frac{\varepsilon}{4},-m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\}.

Proof.

By Lemma
21
, it suffices to
show
\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})\leq\varepsilon/8
for (i) and

\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})\geq 0
for (ii). Noting
\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}

is non-decreasing, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})=\widetilde{\mu}_{l}[\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1}))]\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{l}[\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})+\delta_{1}(\varepsilon))-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1}))]

which yields (i) by continuity of
\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
from Lemma
18
. For (ii), non-decreasing
\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
again implies

\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1})=\widetilde{\mu}_{l}[\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t_{1}))]\geq 0.

∎

Next, we bound the differences when the age process has a negative jump due to
a job’s departure following service completion.

Lemma 23

(Size of a negative jump of P
c\mu
index)
.

There exists
N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for
n\geq N(\varepsilon)

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t^{-})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l}^{n}(t)|\leq\min\Big\{\frac{\varepsilon}{4},m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big\}.

Proof.

By Lemma
21
, it suffices to
show

\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t^{-})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t)\leq\min\{\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\frac{m\beta_{0}}{2}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\}.

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t)\neq\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(t^{-})

only arises when a job from the predicted class
l
completes service and
leaves the system at time
t
. By definition, the age process will incur a
negative jump that corresponds to the interarrival time of two consecutive
jobs. It follows from Proposition
3
that

|\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t^{-})|\leq n^{-1/2}\sup_{1\leq i\leq\widetilde{A}_{l}^{n}(n)}\widetilde{u}_{li}^{n}\leq\min\{\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),\delta_{2}(\varepsilon)\}.

for all sufficently large
n
. Combining the above and continuity of

\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{l}
from Lemma
18
,

|\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t)-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{l}^{n}(t^{-})|=\widetilde{\mu}_{l}|\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t))-\widetilde{C}_{l}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{l}^{n}(t^{-}))|\leq\min\Big\{\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\frac{m\beta_{0}}{2}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\Big\}.

∎

F.2
Proof of Proposition
8

Without loss of generality, we fix
\varepsilon>0
,
n>N(\varepsilon)
,
and
t_{1}\in[0,1-\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)]
. For simplicity, let

t_{2}=t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
. Choose any
l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]
. By
symmetry, it suffices to show that

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})<\varepsilon
. Let
s^{n}_{0}

denote the largest (scaled) time point in
[t_{1},t_{2}]
at which the predicted
class
l_{2}
is selected by P
c\mu
-rule

s^{n}_{0}:=\sup\left\{t\mid t\in[t_{1},t_{2}],~\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(t)=\max_{l\in[K]}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l}(t)\right\}.

We can obtain from the definition of P
c\mu
-rule that

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})=\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})]}_{\text{by Lemmas~\ref{lemma: continuity of gcmu index} and~\ref{lemma: size of gcmu's negative jump}, }\leq\varepsilon/2}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})]}_{\text{by P$c\mu$-rule, }\leq 0}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})]}_{\text{by Lemmas~\ref{lemma: continuity of gcmu index}, }\leq\varepsilon/2}.

The second term satisfies

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{0})\leq 0
since predicted class

l_{2}
is selected for service by P
c\mu
-rule at time
s^{n}_{0}
. The other two
terms can be bounded by
\varepsilon/2
due to our selection of
t_{2}
and
continuity of P
c\mu
index, as show in Lemmas
22
and
23
. In particular, the
first term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(s^{n}_{0})=\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}((s^{n}_{0})^{-})]}_{\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma: continuity of gcmu index}, }\leq\varepsilon/4}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}((s^{n}_{0})^{-})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(s^{n}_{0})]}_{\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma: size of gcmu's negative jump}, }\leq\varepsilon/4}\leq\varepsilon/2,

since
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{1}}(s^{n}_{0})\leq n^{1/2}(t_{2}-s^{n}_{0})\leq\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
. Similarly, the third term satisfies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(s^{n}_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(t_{2})\leq\varepsilon/2,

by Lemma
22
, since
l_{2}
is not served
on the scaled interval
[s^{n}_{0},t_{2}]
, and thus

\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(s^{n}_{0})\geq 0
.

F.3
Proof of Proposition
9

Let
t_{2}=t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
. By symmetry, it suffices to show

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})<\varepsilon
for

l_{1},l_{2}\in[K]
. First, consider the scenario (ii) where idling occurs in

[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
, i.e.,

\sum_{l}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{2})-\sum_{l}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1})<n\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
.
Since we only consider work conserving policies, idling implies that there is
no job in queue at some time
ns^{n}_{2}\in[nt_{1},nt_{2}]
. Consequently, the age
of all predicted classes is zero
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l}(s^{n}_{2})=0,~\forall~l\in[K]
. Then,

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})=\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{2})]}_{\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma: continuity of gcmu index}, }\leq\varepsilon/2}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{2})]}_{\text{by definition, }=0}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})]}_{\text{by $\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(s^{n}_{2})=0$, }\leq 0}\leq\varepsilon,

since
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{2})\geq 0
.

The case (i) where no idling occurs is more complicated. We begin by showing
that the age and the P
c\mu
index decrease sufficiently. See
Section
F.3.1
for the proof of the following result.

Lemma 24

(Sufficient descent in age process)
.

For all
t_{1}\in[0,1-\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)]
, assume

(i)

(Non-Selected Class) at least one predicted class, say
l^{n}_{0}
, is not
selected by P
c\mu
-rule in time interval

[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon))]
;

(ii)

(No Idling)

\sum_{l}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)))-\sum_{l}\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1})=n\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)
.

There exists
N(\varepsilon)
such that for all
n>N(\varepsilon)
, there
is a predicted class
k^{n}_{0}
whose age process decreases sufficiently:

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1}+\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon))-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
.

Let
k^{n}_{0}
be the predicted class with

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).
Let
s^{n}_{1}
denote the smallest scaled time in

[t_{1},t_{2}]
at which
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}
experience such decrease

s^{n}_{1}:=\inf\{t\mid t\in[t_{1},t_{2}],~\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t)-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\}.

If predicted class
l_{2}
is selected for service by P
c\mu
-rule in

[nt_{1},ns^{n}_{1}]
, we can show

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s_{1}^{n})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})\leq\varepsilon
by a similar
analysis as the proof of Proposition
8
. The crux of
our proof lies in the scenario where
l_{2}
is not selected in

[nt_{1},ns^{n}_{1}]
. At
s^{n}_{1}
,
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}
has a negative jump by a
service completion in predicted class
k^{n}_{0}
and the P
c\mu
index decreases
sufficiently.

Lemma 25

(Sufficient descent in P
c\mu
index)
.

For any
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq 1
, assume there exists some predicted class

k^{n}_{0}
satisfying

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
.
There exists
N(\varepsilon)>0
such that for any
n\geq N(\varepsilon)
,
the P
c\mu
index for this predicted class decreases sufficiently

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-3m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).

Proof.

By Lemma
21
, it suffices to
show

\displaystyle\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})=\widetilde{\mu}_{k^{n}_{0}}\left(\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{k^{n}_{0}}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{k^{n}_{0}}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(t_{1}))\right)\leq-4m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).

Since
\widetilde{C}_{k^{n}_{0}}
is strongly convex,

\displaystyle[\widetilde{C}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1}))][\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})]\geq m[\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})]^{2}.

Then,
\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)
yields

\widetilde{C}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{\prime}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1}))\leq m[\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})]\leq-2m\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon),

and

\displaystyle\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{2})-\underline{\bar{\mathcal{I}}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})=\widetilde{\mu}_{k^{n}_{0}}[\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{k^{n}_{0}}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(t_{2}))-\widetilde{C}^{\prime}_{k^{n}_{0}}(\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(t_{1}))]\leq-2\widetilde{\mu}_{\min}m\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)=-4m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).

∎

By Lemma
25
, we have

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-3m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).
Assume first that
l_{1}\neq k^{n}_{0}
. Then

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}((s^{n}_{1})^{-})=\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})
because predicted class

l_{1}
is not served at
s^{n}_{1}
. Consequently, there is no negative jump of the
index and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s_{1}^{n})=

\displaystyle\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}((s^{n}_{1})^{-})]}_{\text{no negative jump at }s^{n}_{1},~=0}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}((s^{n}_{1})^{-})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}((s^{n}_{1})^{-})]}_{\text{by P$c\mu$-rule, }\leq 0}

(70)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}((s^{n}_{1})^{-})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})]}_{\text{by Lemma~\ref{lemma: size of gcmu's negative jump}, }\leq m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)}+\underbrace{[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})]}_{\leq-3m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)}+\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})-m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).

For
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})
, since
l_{2}
is NOT selected by the P
c\mu
-rule in
[nt_{1},ns^{n}_{1}]
,
\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(s^{n}_{1})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}^{n}_{l_{2}}(t_{1})\geq 0
, which yields

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})\geq\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{1})-m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)

(71)

by Lemma
22
. By the condition in the proposition, subtracting (
71
) from (
70
) yields

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq\varepsilon.

If instead
l_{1}=k^{n}_{0}
, then combining the descent

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-3m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)

of Lemma
25
with (
71
) yields the
same bound with additional slack,

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{1}}^{n}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{l_{2}}^{n}(t_{1})-2m\beta_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)\leq\varepsilon.

To show
s^{n}_{1}\geq t_{1}+\gamma_{0}\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)
, recall from the
choice of
s^{n}_{1}
that

\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(s^{n}_{1})-\bm{\widetilde{a}}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(t_{1})\leq-2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon).

Then, by Lemmas
19
,
20
, it is easy to verify that for sufficiently large
n

\displaystyle n(s^{n}_{1}-t_{1})\geq\widetilde{T}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(ns^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{T}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(nt_{1})\geq

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{\rho}_{k^{n}_{0}}[n^{1/2}(s^{n}_{1}-t_{1})+2\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)-\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle n^{1/2}\cdot\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}[n^{1/2}(s^{n}_{1}-t_{1})+\alpha_{0}\delta_{1}(\varepsilon)],

where
\widetilde{T}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(ns^{n}_{1})-\widetilde{T}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}(nt_{1})\leq n(s^{n}_{1}-t_{1})

follows from the definition of the policy process
\widetilde{T}^{n}_{k^{n}_{0}}
. This yields
the desired result that

s^{n}_{1}-t_{1}\geq\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}\alpha_{0}}{1-\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}}\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)=\gamma_{0}\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)
. Note that

\gamma_{0}\in(0,1)
because the critical load condition

\sum_{k}\rho_{k}=1
in Assumption
2
implies

\rho_{\text{min}}\leq\frac{1}{K}
,
\rho_{\text{max}}\geq\frac{1}{K}
,
and
1-\rho_{\min}\geq(K-1)/K
, giving

\gamma_{0}\leq 1/[3(K-1)^{2}]\leq 1/3<1
for
K\geq 2
.

F.3.1
Proof of Lemma
24

By condition (i), it is clear that

\widetilde{T}_{l^{n}_{0}}^{n}(nt_{1}+n\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))-\widetilde{T}_{l^{n}_{0}}^{n}(nt_{1})=0
,
since the predicted class
l^{n}_{0}
is not selected by P
c\mu
-rule in
[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))]
. Intuitively, the server is busy for serving other predicted classes, implying positive stochastic fluctuations of the policy processes dedicated to the other predicted classes, and there must be at least one predicted classes that absorbs the additional service. In particular, we claim that there exists some predicted class
k^{n}_{0}
such that

\widetilde{T}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)))-\widetilde{T}_{k^{n}_{0}}^{n}(nt_{1})\geq\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\rho}_{\min}}{K-1}+\widetilde{\rho}_{k^{n}_{0}}\Big)\cdot n\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon).

(72)

For simplicity, for all
l\in[K]
, let

\Delta\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1}):=\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n}(n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon)))-\widetilde{T}_{l}^{n}(nt_{1}),\quad w^{n}_{l}:=\Delta\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1})/(n\delta^{n}_{1}(\varepsilon)),

where
\Delta\widetilde{T}^{n}_{l}(nt_{1})
represents the service time allocated to predicted class
l
during
[nt_{1},n(t_{1}+\delta_{1}^{n}(\varepsilon))]
, and
w^{n}_{l}
denotes its proportion in the time interval. According to conditions (i) and (ii) and Assumption
2
